Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

180: +package core
181: +
182: +import (
183: +	"context"
184: +	"errors"
185: +	"fmt"
186: +	"net"
187: +	"os/exec"
188: +
189: +	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
190: +	"github.com/containernetworking/plugins/pkg/ns"
191: +	clablinks "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/links"
192: +	clabnetem "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/netem"
193: +)
194: +
195: +func linksEndpoints(links map[int]clablinks.Link) []clablinks.Endpoint {
196: +	var endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint
197: +	for _, link := range links {
198: +		endpoints = append(endpoints, link.GetEndpoints()...)
199: +	}
200: +
201: +	return endpoints
202: +}
203: +
204: +// applyLinkImpairments sets the link impairments of the given endpoints on their interfaces.
205: +// Impairments don't survive moving an interface to another namespace, so they have to be set
206: +// again whenever the interfaces of a node are restored.
207: +func applyLinkImpairments(ctx context.Context, endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint) error {
208: +	var errs []error
209: +	moduleLoaded := false
210: +
211: +	for _, endpoint := range endpoints {
212: +		netem := endpoint.GetNetem()
213: +		if netem == nil {
214: +			continue
215: +		}
216: +
217: +		if !moduleLoaded {
218: +			loadNetemModule(ctx)
219: +			moduleLoaded = true
220: +		}
221: +
222: +		if err := applyEndpointImpairment(ctx, endpoint, netem); err != nil {
223: +			errs = append(
224: +				errs,
225: +				fmt.Errorf("failed to set the link impairment of %s: %w", endpoint, err),
226: +			)
227: +		}
228: +	}
229: +
230: +	return errors.Join(errs...)
231: +}
232: +
233: +func applyEndpointImpairment(
234: +	ctx context.Context,
235: +	endpoint clablinks.Endpoint,
236: +	netem *clablinks.Netem,
237: +) error {
238: +	node := endpoint.GetNode()
239: +
240: +	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
241: +		nodeNS, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
242: +		if err != nil {
243: +			return err
244: +		}
245: +		defer nodeNS.Close()
246: +
247: +		tcnl, err := clabnetem.NewTC(int(nodeNS.Fd()))
248: +		if err != nil {
249: +			return err
250: +		}
251: +		defer func() {
252: +			if err := tcnl.Close(); err != nil {
253: +				log.Debugf("could not close rtnetlink socket: %v", err)
254: +			}
255: +		}()
256: +
257: +		link, err := net.InterfaceByName(endpoint.GetIfaceName())
258: +		if err != nil {
259: +			return err
260: +		}
261: +
262: +		if err := clabnetem.ReplaceImpairments(
263: +			tcnl,
264: +			link,
265: +			netem.Delay,
266: +			netem.Jitter,
267: +			netem.Loss,
268: +			netem.Rate,
269: +			netem.Corruption,
270: +		); err != nil {
271: +			return err
272: +		}
273: +
274: +		log.Info(
275: +			"Set link impairments",
276: +			"node", node.GetShortName(),
277: +			"interface", endpoint.GetIfaceName(),
278: +		)
279: +
280: +		return nil
281: +	})
282: +}
283: +
284: +// loadNetemModule loads the sch_netem kernel module, which some distributions don't load on
285: +// their own.
286: +func loadNetemModule(ctx context.Context) {
287: +	if err := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "modprobe", "sch_netem").Run(); err != nil {
288: +		log.Debug("failed to load the sch_netem kernel module", "err", err)
289: +	}
290: +}

327: diff --git a/docs/manual/impairments.md b/docs/manual/impairments.md
328: index 4960d7b..12b02d6 100644
329: --- a/docs/manual/impairments.md
330: +++ b/docs/manual/impairments.md
331: @@ -16,5 +16,49 @@ Labs are meant to be a reflection of real-world scenarios. To make simulated net
332:  These commands allow users to set, show and reset link impairments (delay, jitter, packet loss) on any link that belongs to a container node and create labs simulating real-world network conditions.
333:  
334:  ```bash title="setting packet loss at 10% rate on eth1 interface of clab-netem-r1 node"
335:  containerlab tools netem set -n clab-netem-r1 -i eth1 --loss 10
336:  ```
337: +
338: +## Impairments in the topology
339: +
340: +Impairments can also be part of the topology, so they are set whenever the lab is deployed. The `netem` key of a link endpoint takes the same impairments as `tools netem set`, which apply to the packets the endpoint's interface sends:
341: +
342: +```yaml
343: +topology:
344: +  links:
345: +    - type: veth
346: +      endpoints:
347: +        - node: r1
348: +          interface: eth1
349: +          netem:
350: +            delay: 50ms
351: +            jitter: 5ms
352: +            loss: 0.5
353: +        - node: r2
354: +          interface: eth1
355: +          netem:
356: +            rate: 10000
357: +```
358: +
359: +| Key          | Meaning                                                               |
360: +| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
361: +| `delay`      | Time to delay the packets, e.g. `100ms`                               |
362: +| `jitter`     | Delay variation, e.g. `10ms`; needs a `delay`, at most `2.147483647s` |
363: +| `loss`       | Random packet loss in percent                                         |
364: +| `rate`       | Rate limit in kbit/s                                                  |
365: +| `corruption` | Random packet corruption in percent                                   |
366: +
367: +Brief links take a list with the impairment of each endpoint, in the order of the endpoints. An empty entry leaves its endpoint unimpaired:
368: +
369: +```yaml
370: +topology:
371: +  links:
372: +    - endpoints: ["r1:eth1", "r2:eth1"]
373: +      netem:
374: +        - delay: 50ms
375: +        - {}
376: +```
377: +
378: +Impairments are supported on the node endpoints of veth links; other link types and `host` or `mgmt-net` endpoints reject them. Lab runtimes such as [clabernetes](clabernetes/runtime.md) don't apply them yet, so deploying a topology with impairments there fails.
379: +
380: +Containerlab sets them again when a node is started or restarted, since a node's interfaces lose their impairments when it is stopped, and when the lab is reconciled with a changed topology. `tools netem` can still change them while the lab runs, until containerlab sets the topology's impairments again. Removing an impairment from the topology doesn't remove it from a running lab; use `tools netem reset` for that.
381: diff --git a/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md b/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md
382: index eccf1ab..d29cb3a 100644

390:          ipv6: <NodeA-IPv6-Address>          # optional e.g. 2001:db8::1/64

400:      labels: <link-labels>                   # optional (used in templating)
401:  ```
402:  
403: +The `netem` key sets [link impairments](impairments.md#impairments-in-the-topology) on the interface of an endpoint.

695: index 0000000..ea991e2

699: +package links
700: +
701: +import (
702: +	"fmt"
703: +	"math"
704: +	"slices"
705: +	"time"
706: +)
707: +
708: +// maxNetemJitter is the highest jitter the kernel accepts without capping it.
709: +const maxNetemJitter = math.MaxInt32 * time.Nanosecond
710: +
711: +// maxNetemRate is the highest rate in kbit/s that can be converted to bytes/s.
712: +const maxNetemRate = math.MaxUint64 / 1000
713: +
714: +// NetemRaw is the link impairment of an endpoint as defined in the topology file.
715: +// It applies to the packets the endpoint's interface sends.
716: +type NetemRaw struct {
717: +	Delay      string  `yaml:"delay,omitempty"`
718: +	Jitter     string  `yaml:"jitter,omitempty"`
719: +	Loss       float64 `yaml:"loss,omitempty"`
720: +	Rate       uint64  `yaml:"rate,omitempty"`
721: +	Corruption float64 `yaml:"corruption,omitempty"`
722: +}
723: +
724: +// Netem is the resolved link impairment of an endpoint.
725: +type Netem struct {
726: +	Delay      time.Duration
727: +	Jitter     time.Duration
728: +	Loss       float64
729: +	Rate       uint64
730: +	Corruption float64
731: +}
732: +
733: +// Resolve validates the impairment and returns nil if it sets nothing.
734: +func (r *NetemRaw) Resolve() (*Netem, error) {
735: +	if r == nil {
736: +		return nil, nil
737: +	}
738: +
739: +	netem := &Netem{Loss: r.Loss, Rate: r.Rate, Corruption: r.Corruption}
740: +
741: +	var err error
742: +	if netem.Delay, err = parseNetemDuration("delay", r.Delay); err != nil {
743: +		return nil, err
744: +	}
745: +	if netem.Jitter, err = parseNetemDuration("jitter", r.Jitter); err != nil {
746: +		return nil, err
747: +	}
748: +
749: +	if netem.Jitter != 0 && netem.Delay == 0 {
750: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter cannot be set without setting delay")
751: +	}
752: +	if netem.Jitter > maxNetemJitter {
753: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter must be at most %s", maxNetemJitter)
754: +	}
755: +	if !(netem.Loss >= 0 && netem.Loss <= 100) {
756: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem loss must be in the range between 0 and 100")
757: +	}
758: +	if !(netem.Corruption >= 0 && netem.Corruption <= 100) {
759: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100")
760: +	}
761: +
762: +	if netem.Rate > maxNetemRate {
763: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem rate must be at most %d kbit/s", uint64(maxNetemRate))
764: +	}
765: +
766: +	if *netem == (Netem{}) {
767: +		return nil, nil
768: +	}
769: +
770: +	return netem, nil
771: +}
772: +
773: +func parseNetemDuration(name, value string) (time.Duration, error) {
774: +	if value == "" {
775: +		return 0, nil
776: +	}
777: +
778: +	duration, err := time.ParseDuration(value)
779: +	if err != nil {
780: +		return 0, fmt.Errorf("netem %s %q is not a duration, e.g. 100ms: %w", name, value, err)
781: +	}
782: +	if duration < 0 {
783: +		return 0, fmt.Errorf("netem %s %q must not be negative", name, value)
784: +	}
785: +
786: +	return duration, nil
787: +}
788: +
789: +func declaresNetem(e *EndpointRaw) bool {
790: +	return e != nil && e.Netem != nil && *e.Netem != (NetemRaw{})
791: +}
792: +
793: +// checkNetemOnVeth rejects link impairments on the endpoints of a link that is not a veth link.
794: +func checkNetemOnVeth(lt LinkType, endpoints ...*EndpointRaw) error {
795: +	if slices.ContainsFunc(endpoints, declaresNetem) {
796: +		return fmt.Errorf("netem is only supported on veth links, not on %s links", lt)
797: +	}
798: +
799: +	return nil
800: +}
801: +
802: +// DeclaresNetem reports whether the link sets a link impairment on any of its endpoints.
803: +func (ld *LinkDefinition) DeclaresNetem() bool {
804: +	var endpoints []*EndpointRaw
805: +
806: +	switch l := ld.Link.(type) {
807: +	case *LinkVEthRaw:
808: +		endpoints = l.Endpoints
809: +	case *LinkVEthStitchedRaw:
810: +		endpoints = l.Endpoints
811: +	case *LinkHostRaw:
812: +		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
813: +	case *LinkMacVlanRaw:
814: +		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
815: +	case *LinkMgmtNetRaw:
816: +		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
817: +	case *LinkDummyRaw:
818: +		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
819: +	case *LinkVxlanRaw:
820: +		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{&l.Endpoint}
821: +	}
822: +
823: +	return slices.ContainsFunc(endpoints, declaresNetem)
824: +}

843: +func TestNetemRawResolve(t *testing.T) {
844: +	tests := map[string]struct {
845: +		raw     *NetemRaw
846: +		want    *Netem
847: +		wantErr string
848: +	}{
849: +		"nothing set": {
850: +			raw: &NetemRaw{},
851: +		},
852: +		"no impairment": {
853: +			raw: nil,
854: +		},
855: +		"every impairment": {
856: +			raw: &NetemRaw{Delay: "50ms", Jitter: "5ms", Loss: 0.5, Rate: 10000, Corruption: 0.1},
857: +			want: &Netem{
858: +				Delay:      50 * time.Millisecond,
859: +				Jitter:     5 * time.Millisecond,
860: +				Loss:       0.5,
861: +				Rate:       10000,
862: +				Corruption: 0.1,
863: +			},
864: +		},
865: +		"delay that is not a duration": {
866: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "50"},
867: +			wantErr: `netem delay "50" is not a duration`,
868: +		},
869: +		"negative jitter": {
870: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "50ms", Jitter: "-5ms"},
871: +			wantErr: "must not be negative",
872: +		},
873: +		"jitter without delay": {
874: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Jitter: "5ms"},
875: +			wantErr: "jitter cannot be set without setting delay",
876: +		},
877: +		"jitter the kernel would cap": {
878: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "5m", Jitter: "1m"},
879: +			wantErr: "netem jitter must be at most 2.147483647s",
880: +		},
881: +		"loss above 100": {
882: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Loss: 101},
883: +			wantErr: "loss must be in the range between 0 and 100",
884: +		},
885: +		"loss that is not a number": {
886: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Loss: math.NaN()},
887: +			wantErr: "loss must be in the range between 0 and 100",
888: +		},
889: +		"rate whose bytes/s overflow": {
890: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Rate: math.MaxUint64},
891: +			wantErr: "netem rate must be at most",
892: +		},
893: +		"negative corruption": {
894: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Corruption: -1},
895: +			wantErr: "corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100",
896: +		},
897: +	}
898: +
899: +	for name, tc := range tests {
900: +		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
901: +			got, err := tc.raw.Resolve()
902: +			if tc.wantErr != "" {
903: +				if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tc.wantErr) {
904: +					t.Fatalf("want error containing %q, got %v", tc.wantErr, err)
905: +				}
906: +				return
907: +			}
908: +			if err != nil {
909: +				t.Fatal(err)
910: +			}
911: +			if d := cmp.Diff(tc.want, got); d != "" {
912: +				t.Fatalf("netem mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", d)
913: +			}
914: +		})
915: +	}
916: +}

1310: +func TestNetemDurationPattern(t *testing.T) {
1311: +	b, err := os.ReadFile("clab.schema.json")
1312: +	if err != nil {
1313: +		t.Fatal(err)
1314: +	}
1315: +	var schema struct {
1316: +		Definitions map[string]struct {
1317: +			Properties map[string]struct {
1318: +				Pattern string
1319: +			}
1320: +		}
1321: +	}
1322: +	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &schema); err != nil {
1323: +		t.Fatal(err)
1324: +	}
1325: +	pattern := regexp.MustCompile(schema.Definitions["netem"].Properties["delay"].Pattern)
1326: +	for _, tc := range []struct {
1327: +		duration string
1328: +		valid    bool
1329: +	}{
1330: +		{duration: "100ms", valid: true},
1331: +		{duration: "1.5s", valid: true},
1332: +		{duration: "1m30s", valid: true},
1333: +		{duration: "250µs", valid: true},
1334: +		{duration: "250us", valid: true},
1335: +		{duration: "100"},
1336: +		{duration: "-5ms"},
1337: +		{duration: "5 ms"},
1338: +	} {
1339: +		t.Run(tc.duration, func(t *testing.T) {
1340: +			if got := pattern.MatchString(tc.duration); got != tc.valid {
1341: +				t.Errorf("schema accepts = %v, want %v", got, tc.valid)
1342: +			}
1343: +			d, err := time.ParseDuration(tc.duration)
1344: +			if tc.valid && (err != nil || d < 0) {
1345: +				t.Errorf("the schema accepts %q, but the topology doesn't: %v", tc.duration, err)
1346: +			}
1347: +		})
1348: +	}
1349: +}

index 4960d7b..12b02d6 100644\n329: --- a/docs/manual/impairments.md\n330: +++ b/docs/manual/impairments.md\n331: @@ -16,5 +16,49 @@ Labs are meant to be a reflection of real-world scenarios. To make simulated net\n332:  These commands allow users to set, show and reset link impairments (delay, jitter, packet loss) on any link that belongs to a container node and create labs simulating real-world network conditions.\n333:  \n334:  ```bash title=\"setting packet loss at 10% rate on eth1 interface of clab-netem-r1 node\"\n335:  containerlab tools netem set -n clab-netem-r1 -i eth1 --loss 10\n336:  ```\n337: +\n338: +## Impairments in the topology\n339: +\n340: +Impairments can also be part of the topology, so they are set whenever the lab is deployed. The `netem` key of a link endpoint takes the same impairments as `tools netem set`, which apply to the packets the endpoint's interface sends:\n341: +\n342: +```yaml\n343: +topology:\n344: +  links:\n345: +    - type: veth\n346: +      endpoints:\n347: +        - node: r1\n348: +          interface: eth1\n349: +          netem:\n350: +            delay: 50ms\n351: +            jitter: 5ms\n352: +            loss: 0.5\n353: +        - node: r2\n354: +          interface: eth1\n355: +          netem:\n356: +            rate: 10000\n357: +```\n358: +\n359: +| Key          | Meaning                                                               |\n360: +| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n361: +| `delay`      | Time to delay the packets, e.g. `100ms`                               |\n362: +| `jitter`     | Delay variation, e.g. `10ms`; needs a `delay`, at most `2.147483647s` |\n363: +| `loss`       | Random packet loss in percent                                         |\n364: +| `rate`       | Rate limit in kbit/s                                                  |\n365: +| `corruption` | Random packet corruption in percent                                   |\n366: +\n367: +Brief links take a list with the impairment of each endpoint, in the order of the endpoints. An empty entry leaves its endpoint unimpaired:\n368: +\n369: +```yaml\n370: +topology:\n371: +  links:\n372: +    - endpoints: [\"r1:eth1\", \"r2:eth1\"]\n373: +      netem:\n374: +        - delay: 50ms\n375: +        - {}\n376: +```\n377: +\n378: +Impairments are supported on the node endpoints of veth links; other link types and `host` or `mgmt-net` endpoints reject them. Lab runtimes such as [clabernetes](clabernetes/runtime.md) don't apply them yet, so deploying a topology with impairments there fails.\n379: +\n380: +Containerlab sets them again when a node is started or restarted, since a node's interfaces lose their impairments when it is stopped, and when the lab is reconciled with a changed topology. `tools netem` can still change them while the lab runs, until containerlab sets the topology's impairments again. Removing an impairment from the topology doesn't remove it from a running lab; use `tools netem reset` for that.\n381: diff --git a/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md b/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md\n382: a/docs/manual/impairments.md\n+++ b/docs/manual/impairments.md\n@@ -16,5 +16,49 @@ Labs are meant to be a reflection of real-world scenarios. To make simulated net\n These commands allow users to set, show and reset link impairments (delay, jitter, packet loss) on any link that belongs to a container node and create labs simulating real-world network conditions.\n \n ```bash title=\"setting packet loss at 10% rate on eth1 interface of clab-netem-r1 node\"\n containerlab tools netem set -n clab-netem-r1 -i eth1 --loss 10\n ```\n+\n+## Impairments in the topology\n+\n+Impairments can also be part of the topology, so they are set whenever the lab is deployed. The `netem` key of a link endpoint takes the same impairments as `tools netem set`, which apply to the packets the endpoint's interface sends:\n+\n+```yaml\n+topology:\n+  links:\n+    - type: veth\n+      endpoints:\n+        - node: r1\n+          interface: eth1\n+          netem:\n+            delay: 50ms\n+            jitter: 5ms\n+            loss: 0.5\n+        - node: r2\n+          interface: eth1\n+          netem:\n+            rate: 10000\n+```\n+\n+| Key          | Meaning                                                               |\n+| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| `delay`      | Time to delay the packets, e.g. `100ms`                               |\n+| `jitter`     | Delay variation, e.g. `10ms`; needs a `delay`, at most `2.147483647s` |\n+| `loss`       | Random packet loss in percent                                         |\n+| `rate`       | Rate limit in kbit/s                                                  |\n+| `corruption` | Random packet corruption in percent                                   |\n+\n+Brief links take a list with the impairment of each endpoint, in the order of the endpoints. An empty entry leaves its endpoint unimpaired:\n+\n+```yaml\n+topology:\n+  links:\n+    - endpoints: [\"r1:eth1\", \"r2:eth1\"]\n+      netem:\n+        - delay: 50ms\n+        - {}\n+```\n+\n+Impairments are supported on the node endpoints of veth links; other link types and `host` or `mgmt-net` endpoints reject them. Lab runtimes such as [clabernetes](clabernetes/runtime.md) don't apply them yet, so deploying a topology with impairments there fails.\n+\n+Containerlab sets them again when a node is started or restarted, since a node's interfaces lose their impairments when it is stopped, and when the lab is reconciled with a changed topology. `tools netem` can still change them while the lab runs, until containerlab sets the topology's impairments again. Removing an impairment from the topology doesn't remove it from a running lab; use `tools netem reset` for that.\ndiff --git a/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md b/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md\nindex accepts without capping it.\n+const maxNetemJitter = math.MaxInt32 * time.Nanosecond\n+\n+// maxNetemRate is the highest rate in kbit/s that can be converted to bytes/s.\n+const maxNetemRate = math.MaxUint64 / 1000\n+\n+// NetemRaw is the link impairment of an endpoint as defined in the topology file.\n+// It applies to the packets the endpoint's interface sends.\n+type NetemRaw struct {\n+\tDelay      string  `yaml:\"delay,omitempty\"`\n+\tJitter     string  `yaml:\"jitter,omitempty\"`\n+\tLoss       float64 `yaml:\"loss,omitempty\"`\n+\tRate       uint64  `yaml:\"rate,omitempty\"`\n+\tCorruption float64 `yaml:\"corruption,omitempty\"`\n+}\n+\n+// Netem is the resolved link impairment of an endpoint.\n+type Netem struct {\n+\tDelay      time.Duration\n+\tJitter     time.Duration\n+\tLoss       float64\n+\tRate       uint64\n+\tCorruption float64\n+}\n+\n+// Resolve validates the impairment and returns nil if it sets nothing.\n+func (r *NetemRaw) Resolve() (*Netem, error) {\n+\tif r == nil {\n+\t\treturn nil, nil\n+\t}\n+\n+\tnetem := &Netem{Loss: r.Loss, Rate: r.Rate, Corruption: r.Corruption}\n+\n+\tvar err error\n+\tif netem.Delay, err = parseNetemDuration(\"delay\", r.Delay); err != nil {\n+\t\treturn nil, err\n+\t}\n+\tif netem.Jitter, err = parseNetemDuration(\"jitter\", r.Jitter); err != nil {\n+\t\treturn nil, err\n+\t}\n+\n+\tif netem.Jitter != 0 && netem.Delay == 0 {\n+\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"netem jitter cannot be set without setting delay\")\n+\t}\n+\tif netem.Jitter > maxNetemJitter {\n+\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"netem jitter must be at most %s\", maxNetemJitter)\n+\t}\n+\tif !(netem.Loss >= 0 && netem.Loss <= 100) {\n+\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"netem loss must be in the range between 0 and 100\")\n+\t}\n+\tif !(netem.Corruption >= 0 && netem.Corruption <= 100) {\n+\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"netem corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100\")\n+\t}\n+\n+\tif netem.Rate > maxNetemRate {\n+\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"netem rate must be at most %d kbit/s\", uint64(maxNetemRate))\n+\t}\n+\n+\tif *netem == (Netem{}) {\n+\t\treturn nil, nil\n+\t}\n+\n+\treturn netem, nil\n+}\n+\n+func parseNetemDuration(name, value string) (time.Duration, error) {\n+\tif value == \"\" {\n+\t\treturn 0, nil\n+\t}\n+\n+\tduration, err := time.ParseDuration(value)\n+\tif err != nil ../common.robot\n+\n+Suite Setup         Run Clab Command    destroy --name ${lab-name} --cleanup\n+Suite Teardown      Run Clab Command    destroy --name ${lab-name} --cleanup\n+\n+\n+*** Variables <content>
1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"errors"
6: 	"fmt"
7: 	"net"
8: 	"os/exec"
9: 
10: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
11: 	"github.com/containernetworking/plugins/pkg/ns"
12: 	clablinks "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/links"
13: 	clabnetem "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/netem"
14: )
15: 
16: func linksEndpoints(links map[int]clablinks.Link) []clablinks.Endpoint {
17: 	var endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint
18: 	for _, link := range links {
19: 		endpoints = append(endpoints, link.GetEndpoints()...)
20: 	}
21: 
22: 	return endpoints
23: }
24: 
25: // applyLinkImpairments sets the link impairments of the given endpoints on their interfaces.
26: // Impairments don't survive moving an interface to another namespace, so they have to be set
27: // again whenever the interfaces of a node are restored.
28: func applyLinkImpairments(ctx context.Context, endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint) error {
29: 	var errs []error
30: 	moduleLoaded := false
31: 
32: 	for _, endpoint := range endpoints {
33: 		netem := endpoint.GetNetem()
34: 		if netem == nil {
35: 			continue
36: 		}
37: 
38: 		if !moduleLoaded {
39: 			loadNetemModule(ctx)
40: 			moduleLoaded = true
41: 		}
42: 
43: 		if err := applyEndpointImpairment(ctx, endpoint, netem); err != nil {
44: 			errs = append(
45: 				errs,
46: 				fmt.Errorf("failed to set the link impairment of %s: %w", endpoint, err),
47: 			)
48: 		}
49: 	}
50: 
51: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
52: }
53: 
54: func applyEndpointImpairment(
55: 	ctx context.Context,
56: 	endpoint clablinks.Endpoint,
57: 	netem *clablinks.Netem,
58: ) error {
59: 	node := endpoint.GetNode()
60: 
61: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
62: 		nodeNS, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
63: 		if err != nil {
64: 			return err
65: 		}
66: 		defer nodeNS.Close()
67: 
68: 		tcnl, err := clabnetem.NewTC(int(nodeNS.Fd()))
69: 		if err != nil {
70: 			return err
71: 		}
72: 		defer func() {
73: 			if err := tcnl.Close(); err != nil {
74: 				log.Debugf("could not close rtnetlink socket: %v", err)
75: 			}
76: 		}()
77: 
78: 		link, err := net.InterfaceByName(endpoint.GetIfaceName())
79: 		if err != nil {
80: 			return err
81: 		}
82: 
83: 		if err := clabnetem.ReplaceImpairments(
84: 			tcnl,
85: 			link,
86: 			netem.Delay,
87: 			netem.Jitter,
88: 			netem.Loss,
89: 			netem.Rate,
90: 			netem.Corruption,
91: 		); err != nil {
92: 			return err

1: package links
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"fmt"
5: 	"math"
6: 	"slices"
7: 	"time"
8: )
9: 
10: // maxNetemJitter is the highest jitter the kernel accepts without capping it.
11: const maxNetemJitter = math.MaxInt32 * time.Nanosecond
12: 
13: // maxNetemRate is the highest rate in kbit/s that can be converted to bytes/s.
14: const maxNetemRate = math.MaxUint64 / 1000
15: 
16: // NetemRaw is the link impairment of an endpoint as defined in the topology file.
17: // It applies to the packets the endpoint's interface sends.
18: type NetemRaw struct {
19: 	Delay      string  `yaml:"delay,omitempty"`
20: 	Jitter     string  `yaml:"jitter,omitempty"`
21: 	Loss       float64 `yaml:"loss,omitempty"`
22: 	Rate       uint64  `yaml:"rate,omitempty"`
23: 	Corruption float64 `yaml:"corruption,omitempty"`
24: }
25: 
26: // Netem is the resolved link impairment of an endpoint.
27: type Netem struct {
28: 	Delay      time.Duration
29: 	Jitter     time.Duration
30: 	Loss       float64
31: 	Rate       uint64
32: 	Corruption float64
33: }
34: 
35: // Resolve validates the impairment and returns nil if it sets nothing.
36: func (r *NetemRaw) Resolve() (*Netem, error) {
37: 	if r == nil {
38: 		return nil, nil
39: 	}
40: 
41: 	netem := &Netem{Loss: r.Loss, Rate: r.Rate, Corruption: r.Corruption}
42: 
43: 	var err error
44: 	if netem.Delay, err = parseNetemDuration("delay", r.Delay); err != nil {
45: 		return nil, err
46: 	}
47: 	if netem.Jitter, err = parseNetemDuration("jitter", r.Jitter); err != nil {
48: 		return nil, err
49: 	}
50: 
51: 	if netem.Jitter != 0 && netem.Delay == 0 {
52: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter cannot be set without setting delay")
53: 	}
54: 	if netem.Jitter > maxNetemJitter {
55: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter must be at most %s", maxNetemJitter)
56: 	}
57: 	if !(netem.Loss >= 0 && netem.Loss <= 100) {
58: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem loss must be in the range between 0 and 100")
59: 	}
60: 	if !(netem.Corruption >= 0 && netem.Corruption <= 100) {
61: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100")
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	if netem.Rate > maxNetemRate {
65: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem rate must be at most %d kbit/s", uint64(maxNetemRate))
66: 	}
67: 
68: 	if *netem == (Netem{}) {
69: 		return nil, nil
70: 	}
71: 
72: 	return netem, nil
73: }
74: 
75: func parseNetemDuration(name, value string) (time.Duration, error) {
76: 	if value == "" {
77: 		return 0, nil
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	duration, err := time.ParseDuration(value)
81: 	if err != nil {
82: 		return 0, fmt.Errorf("netem %s %q is not a duration, e.g. 100ms: %w", name, value, err)
83: 	}
84: 	if duration < 0 {
85: 		return 0, fmt.Errorf("netem %s %q must not be negative", name, value)
86: 	}
87: 
88: 	return duration, nil
89: }
90: 
91: func declaresNetem(e *EndpointRaw) bool {
92: 	return e != nil && e.Netem != nil && *e.Netem != (NetemRaw{})
93: }
94: 
95: // checkNetemOnVeth rejects link impairments on the endpoints of a link that is not a veth link.
96: func checkNetemOnVeth(lt LinkType, endpoints ...*EndpointRaw) error {
97: 	if slices.ContainsFunc(endpoints, declaresNetem) {
98: 		return fmt.Errorf("netem is only supported on veth links, not on %s links", lt)
99: 	}
100: 
101: 	return nil
102: }
103: 
104: // DeclaresNetem reports whether the link sets a link impairment on any of its endpoints.
105: func (ld *LinkDefinition) DeclaresNetem() bool {
106: 	var endpoints []*EndpointRaw
107: 
108: 	switch l := ld.Link.(type) {
109: 	case *LinkVEthRaw:
110: 		endpoints = l.Endpoints
111: 	case *LinkVEthStitchedRaw:
112: 		endpoints = l.Endpoints
113: 	case *LinkHostRaw:
114: 		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
115: 	case *LinkMacVlanRaw:
116: 		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
117: 	case *LinkMgmtNetRaw:
118: 		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
119: 	case *LinkDummyRaw:
120: 		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{l.Endpoint}
121: 	case *LinkVxlanRaw:
122: 		endpoints = []*EndpointRaw{&l.Endpoint}
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	return slices.ContainsFunc(endpoints, declaresNetem)
126: }

49: // Resolving a veth link resolves its endpoints.
50: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams) (Link, error) {
51: 	// filtered true means the link is in the filter provided by a user
52: 	// aka it should be resolved/created/deployed

185: func (l *LinkVEth) deployAEnd(ctx context.Context, idx int) error {
186: 	ep := l.Endpoints[idx]
187: 	// the peer Endpoint is the other of the two endpoints in the
188: 	// Endpoints slice. So do a +1 on the index and modulo operation
189: 	// to take care of the wrap around.
190: 	peerIdx := (idx + 1) % 2
191: 	peerEp := l.Endpoints[peerIdx]
192: 
193: 	log.Debugf("Creating Endpoint: %s ( --> %s )", ep, peerEp)
194: 
195: 	// build the netlink.Veth struct for the link provisioning
196: 	linkA := &netlink.Veth{
197: 		LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{
198: 			Name: ep.GetRandIfaceName(),
199: 			MTU:  l.MTU,
200: 			// Mac address is set later on
201: 		},
202: 		PeerName: peerEp.GetRandIfaceName(),
203: 		// PeerMac address is set later on
204: 	}
205: 
206: 	// add the link
207: 	err := netlink.LinkAdd(linkA)
208: 	if err != nil {
209: 		return err
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	// cleanup is a helper that removes the veth pair if a subsequent step fails
213: 	// after LinkAdd succeeded, preventing orphaned interfaces from blocking retries.
214: 	cleanup := func(err error) error {
215: 		if delErr := netlink.LinkDel(linkA); delErr != nil {
216: 			log.Debugf(
217: 				"failed to cleanup veth pair %s after error: %v",
218: 				ep.GetRandIfaceName(),
219: 				delErr,
220: 			)
221: 		}
222: 		return err
223: 	}
224: 
225: 	// disable TXOffloading
226: 	if err := clabutils.EthtoolTXOff(ep.GetRandIfaceName()); err != nil {
227: 		return cleanup(err)
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	// the link needs to be moved to the relevant network namespace
231: 	// and enabled (up). This is done via linkSetupFunc.
232: 	// based on the endpoint type the link setup function is different.
233: 	// linkSetupFunc is executed in a netns of a node.
234: 	// if the node is a regular namespace node
235: 	// add link to node, rename, set mac and Up
236: 	err = ep.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, linkA,
237: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(linkA, ep))
238: 	if err != nil {
239: 		return cleanup(err)
240: 	}
241: 
242: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed
243: 
244: 	// e.g. host endpoints are nodeless, and therefore the B end of the veth link should
245: 	// be deployed right after the A end is deployed.
246: 	if peerEp.IsNodeless() {
247: 		return l.deployBEnd(ctx, peerIdx)
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	return nil
251: }
252: 
253: func (l *LinkVEth) deployBEnd(ctx context.Context, idx int) error {
254: 	ep := l.Endpoints[idx]
255: 	peerEp := l.Endpoints[(idx+1)%2]
256: 
257: 	log.Debugf("Assigning Endpoint: %s ( --> %s )", ep, peerEp)
258: 
259: 	// retrieve the netlink.Link for the provided Endpoint
260: 	link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ep.GetRandIfaceName())
261: 	if err != nil {
262: 		return err
263: 	}
264: 
265: 	// disable TXOffloading
266: 	if err := clabutils.EthtoolTXOff(ep.GetRandIfaceName()); err != nil {
267: 		return err
268: 	}
269: 
270: 	// Host endpoints already live in the current namespace; set them up in place.
271: 	// Other nodeless endpoints, such as the management bridge, still need their
272: 	// node-specific setup.
273: 	if ep.IsNodeless() && ep.GetNode().GetLinkEndpointType() == LinkEndpointTypeHost {
274: 		if err := SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep)(nil); err != nil {
275: 			return err
276: 		}
277: 	} else if err = ep.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link,
278: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep)); err != nil {
279: 		return err
280: 	}
281: 
282: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed
283: 
284: 	if len(l.Endpoints) == 2 {
285: 		log.Infof("Created link: %s ▪┄┄▪ %s", l.Endpoints[0], l.Endpoints[1])
286: 	}
287: 
288: 	return nil
289: }
290: 
291: // getEndpointIndex returns the index of the ep endpoint belonging to l link.
292: // An error is returned when the ep is not part of the l's endpoints.
293: func (l *LinkVEth) getEndpointIndex(ep Endpoint) (int, error) {
294: 	for idx, e := range l.Endpoints {
295: 		if e == ep {
296: 			return idx, nil
297: 		}
298: 	}
299: 
300: 	// if the endpoint is not part of the link
301: 	// build a string list of endpoints and return a meaningful error
302: 	var epStrings []string
303: 	for _, e := range l.Endpoints {
304: 		epStrings = append(epStrings, e.String())
305: 	}
306: 
307: 	return -1, fmt.Errorf(
308: 		"endpoint %s does not belong to link [ %s ]",
309: 		ep.String(),
310: 		strings.Join(epStrings, ", "),
311: 	)
312: }
313: 
314: // Deploy deploys the veth link by creating the A and B sides of the veth pair independently
315: // based on the calling endpoint.
316: func (l *LinkVEth) Deploy(ctx context.Context, ep Endpoint) error {
317: 	// since each node calls deploy on its links, we need to make sure that we only deploy
318: 	// the link once, even if multiple nodes call deploy on the same link.
319: 	l.deployMutex.Lock()
320: 	defer l.deployMutex.Unlock()
321: 
322: 	// first we need to check that the provided ep is part of this link
323: 	idx, err := l.getEndpointIndex(ep)
324: 	if err != nil {
325: 		return err
326: 	}
327: 
328: 	// Retry transient netlink errors that can occur during concurrent link operations.
329: 	// deployAEnd cleans up partial state on failure, making each retry idempotent.
330: 	return retryTransientNetlink(ctx, fmt.Sprintf("deploy link %s", ep), func() error {
331: 		switch l.DeploymentState {
332: 		case LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed:
333: 			return l.deployBEnd(ctx, idx)
334: 		case LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed:
335: 			return nil
336: 		default:
337: 			return l.deployAEnd(ctx, idx)
338: 		}
339: 	})
340: }
341: 
342: func (*LinkVEth) PostDeploy(context.Context) error {
343: 	return nil
344: }
345: 
346: func (l *LinkVEth) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
347: 	l.deployMutex.Lock()
348: 	defer l.deployMutex.Unlock()
349: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
350: 		return nil
351: 	}
352: 	for _, ep := range l.GetEndpoints() {
353: 		err := ep.Remove(ctx)
354: 		if err != nil {
355: 			log.Debug(err)
356: 		}
357: 	}
358: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateRemoved
359: 	return nil
360: }

363: 	return l.Endpoints
364: }

367: 	return l.GetEndpoints()
